16:T1190,## Cloud apps — multi-tenant hardening & completion before GA

Work order from a deep audit of the **apps / container-deploy / per-tenant-DB** path. The architecture is solid and well-tested in pieces; this is the checklist to take it from *"staging canary (allowlisted orgs)"* to *"safe for self-serve external users."*

**Ground rules for whoever picks this up (cloud agent):**
- Implement + verify **everything in staging** first. The container-deploy and DB-isolation paths only fully exercise when the **provisioning node daemon is armed** — arm it in staging before testing.
- Each item below: re-confirm the exact file:line in the current tree before changing (the audit was a snapshot).
- Land as small, reviewable PRs grouped by the sections below. P1 items are the gate before opening to non-allowlisted users.

---

### P1 — security gates (do first; required before any external / self-serve user)

1. **Rate limiting everywhere it's missing** — global, anonymous-session minting, and the metered inference paths; add sybil control on the free-credit grant. *Today these are unthrottled, so a loop against the anon/inference path is unbounded cost.* **Effort: M**
2. **Enforce the reserved/managed env-var denylist on BOTH container-deploy routes.** The denylist exists but is only applied on one route; the other lets a caller's `environmentVars` override managed keys (e.g. the metered-identity / cloud token). Strip reserved keys on both. **Effort: S**
3. **Centralize outbound fetch behind one IP-pinned SSRF guard** (`safeFetch` that pins the resolved IP for the actual request). Current validate-then-fetch is TOCTOU → DNS-rebinding pivot into the cloud/metadata network. **Effort: M**
4. **Close the Worker-path "isolated DB" silent fallback.** Isolated-DB only truly isolates on the node-daemon path (real `CREATE ROLE`/`REVOKE CONNECT`); the pure-Worker path silently falls back to a shared DB (isolation by UUID only). Either finish the per-tenant DDL TODO in the Worker path, or **hard-fail "isolated" when the daemon isn't armed** — never silently degrade to shared. **Effort: M–L**
5. **Digest-pin the production image allowlist** and tighten the default GHCR orgs — no trailing-`*` namespace trust, no mutable `:latest`. **Effort: S**
6. **CI gate against unscoped tenant DB reads** + run the existing isolation-verify script in CI. Tenant isolation currently rests on every handler remembering to pass `organization_id`; one slip = cross-tenant read. **Effort: S–M**

> The first three (1–3) are the hard gate. Do not open beyond the org allowlist until they're in.

### P2 — completion (make the gated path actually real)

7. **Arm + health-monitor the provisioning daemon as a hard dependency** (alert if down). The container-stop billing cleanup AND real DB isolation both silently break when it isn't running. **Effort: M**
8. **Wire Caddy ingress into the deploy success criteria** so a deploy cannot report success without a reachable public URL. **Effort: S–M**
9. **One end-to-end staging test of the full user chain**: create app → build → isolated DB → live URL. Today it's only covered in disconnected pieces; the "user's own code" build leg is least exercised. **Effort: L**
10. **Finish the consumer payout/redemption side** (currently "temporarily unavailable") and **replace the Cloudflare registrar dev stub** before charging for custom domains in prod. These are live money paths that charge but don't fully deliver yet. **Effort: M**

### P3 — cleanup

11. **Env kill-switch for feature flags** (they're hardcoded `enabled: true` with no runtime override — no way to disable a feature mid-incident). **Effort: S**
12. **Confirm/re-enable the promo duplicate-redemption guard** on the free-credit path (currently commented out → repeat bonus possible). **Effort: S**
13. **Refresh stale comments** (the "no build service yet" header is now wrong) and **re-audit the public-path prefix allowlist**, especially `/api/internal` and `/api/webhooks` that lean on per-handler secret checks (one mis-scoped `startsWith` exposes a subtree). **Effort: S–M**

---

**Bottom line:** strong, well-architected foundation — atomic credit deduction, hashed keys, a real (design-correct) per-tenant DB isolation, prebuilt-image deploy with quota/billing, good unit/integration coverage. The gap is the distance between *"works on the armed-daemon path in staging"* and *"safe for arbitrary external users."* P1 closes that gap.
17:T2ae5,## Summary

On-device inference speed, performance, and memory overhead are first-class product
constraints. We ship a full optimization stack for Eliza-1 — **TurboQuant** weights,
**QJL** K-cache, **PolarQuant / TBQ3** V-cache, **TCQ** trellis quant, **MTP**
speculative decoding, and **fused QJL/Polar attention kernels** — and the
`plugins/plugin-local-inference/native/AGENTS.md` §3 contract says every applicable
optimization is **mandatory, never skipped**.

A runtime audit (Pixel 9a / Mali-G715, plus code trace of the CPU/Vulkan/Metal/CUDA
paths) found that **several of these optimizations are implemented but not actually
applied on the on-device path** — the model is leaving large, already-built wins on
the table. This is a tracking issue to **enforce that every model uses every
optimization it can**, on every backend, and that **any model that can be quantized
and isn't, is**.

Goal: for each (model tier × optimization × backend), the optimization is either
applied or there is a written, verified reason it cannot be — with host-side
numerical verification and on-device perf/overhead numbers recorded.

---

## Current-state audit — the gaps found

### 1. 🔴 Fused QJL/TBQ attention op is implemented + Mali-safe but **never routed** (highest leverage)

`GGML_OP_FUSED_ATTN_QJL_TBQ` is fully implemented end-to-end — node constructor in
`ggml.c`, CPU forward, Vulkan/Metal/CUDA kernels, `supports_op` gate, tests + benches —
and its type contract exactly matches `eliza-1-0_8b`'s KV design (`typeK: qjl1_256`,
`typeV: tbq3_0`). The fused Vulkan kernels are **Mali-safe** (single workgroup of 32,
`barrier()` + shared-mem tree reductions, **zero subgroup intrinsics**, ~1152 B shared
mem) and are **structurally immune to the generic-FA split-K corruption**.

But the graph builder **never constructs it**: `src/llama-graph.cpp:1996-2004`
dequantizes QJL/TBQ/Polar K/V back to F32→F16 and calls generic `ggml_flash_attn_ext`.
So on every backend we run a generic FA over a **dequantized F16 KV cache** — paying a
full double-cast of the KV cache every step and discarding the entire point of the
QJL/Polar quantization.

Routing is **not** a one-line wire — there are **three hard blockers** (full trace in
the audit; the op is referenced only from tests/benches, never from `src/` or the FFI):

- **(dominant, correctness) Q-projection / per-layer Π.** The op consumes a *256-dim
  QJL sketch* Q (`q->ne[0]==256`, `ggml.c:5706`), i.e. `Π·q` with the **same per-layer
  Π that quantized K**. The graph today produces a 128-dim Hadamard-rotated Q (a
  *different* transform), and the K-quant type-trait path uses a single **process-global
  `seed=42` default Π** (`quants-qjl.c:115-135`) while production QJL needs **per-layer Π
  from the training sidecar** (`packages/training/scripts/quantization/qjl_apply.py`).
  So even wiring Q-projection with the default Π would be numerically wrong vs the
  trained KV. Must determine what Π the shipped `eliza-1-0_8b` GGUF was quantized with
  and plumb it into both K-quant and Q-projection.
- **(correctness) non-causal.** The constructor hardcodes `causal=0` (`ggml.c:5725-5733`)
  and the CPU forward has *no* mask logic (sums all `n_kv` tokens). A decoder LLM
  requires causal masking. The Vulkan shader honors a causal push-constant
  (`fused_attn_qjl_tbq.comp:253`) but it's never set. No mask tensor / sinks / ALiBi /
  soft-cap inputs either (Qwen3 needs causal + GQA — GQA *is* supported).
- **(scope) decode-shaped.** The op dispatches one workgroup per `(q_head, q_pos)` and
  `supports_op` forces `ne[2]==ne[3]==1`, so a 1500-token **prefill = 1500 serial
  dispatches** — worse than batched FA on compute-bound Mali. The fused op is the right
  win for **decode** (correctness + parallelism + KV memory + no double-cast), **not**
  for prefill (see #5).
- There is also **no public QJL-K + Polar-V ggml op** — only `qjl_tbq` (which matches
  eliza-1-0_8b's TBQ3-V). The polar Vulkan pipeline is an orphan (no op/constructor/CPU
  forward). eliza-1-0_8b doesn't need it; larger tiers may.

**Win when done:** for the **decode** path — removes the double-cast + F16 KV
materialization, uses the Mali-safe single-pass kernel (never had the split-K bug), and
unlocks quantized KV (#2). Prefill speed is a separate axis (#5). Anchors:
`ggml-vulkan.cpp:775-777,5245-5248,13946-14005,16952-16963`, `ggml.c:5704-5733`,
`fused-attn-qjl-tbq.c`, `ggml.h:2539`, `src/llama-graph.cpp:1990-2008`.

### 2. 🟠 Bionic host runs an F16 KV cache, not the designed QJL/TBQ/Polar cache

The Android Mali GPU path (`eliza_inference_llm_stream_open`,
`tools/omnivoice/src/eliza-inference-ffi.cpp:2879-2884`) maps `cfg.cache_type_k/v` to a
`ggml_type` but the JNI callers `memset` the config to zero → **F16 KV**
(`elizavoice-jni.cpp:1283-1290` `nativeLlmSelfTest`, `:1157-1165` `nativeLlmStreamOpen`).
So the eliza-1 quantized KV cache (QJL-K / TBQ3-V, the memory + bandwidth win) is **not
used on device**.

**Dependency:** do **not** flip `cfg.cache_type` to `qjl1_256/tbq3_0` yet — without #1
it is a *regression* (CPU-offloaded dequant casts + a missing TBQ3_0 V hop). Sequence:
land #1 first, then set the cache types here.

### 3. 🟠 MTP speculative decoding not wired on the bionic host path

`ELIZA_1_LOAD_METADATA` (mobile-device-bridge) declares `mtp: {draftMin:1, draftMax:2}`
for the MTP tiers, but the bionic host builds `cfg` with `draft_min/max = 0` → **MTP
off**. Per AGENTS.md §3, MTP is mandatory where a NextN head exists (2B+). Wire the draft
window through the JNI/`cfg` to the bionic host so on-device decode uses speculative
decoding. (0.8B is the documented non-MTP tier — exempt.)

### 4. 🔴 Generic FA scalar kernel is **non-deterministic** on Mali (intermittent corruption)

Verified on-device: the same prompt under greedy decode (temp=0) produces *different*
outputs run-to-run — ~50% degenerate into `" His!!!!"` token repetition. Identical
deterministic inputs → different outputs = a **race / uninitialized shared memory** in
the Mali FA scalar path (`flash_attn.comp`), **not** split-K (it persists with
`split_k=1`). Current mitigation: FA disabled on Android (reliable but no speedup, since
Mali has no coopmat). Candidate hardening (from the split-K trace) worth testing as the
actual race fix: init `m_max` to `-FLT_MAX` (`uintBitsToFloat(0xFF7FFFFFu)`) not `-inf`,
remove `[[unroll]]` on the runtime-`k_num` loops, clamp `exp()` args with
`max(m-m_max, -87.0)`, and fix the y over-dispatch to `CEIL_DIV(HSV, subgroup_size)`.
**#1 (fused kernel) sidesteps this entirely** for the eliza-1 KV types.

### 5. 🟡 Prefill is matmul-bound on Mali (no cooperative-matrix)

FA-on prefill ≈ FA-off prefill (~22 s / 1500 tokens) → the bottleneck is the
**scalar mul_mat** (QKV proj + FFN), not attention. Mali-G715 exposes no
`VK_KHR_cooperative_matrix`, so the matmul runs the scalar path. Investigate a
Mali-tuned `mul_mm` (warp-tile, f16-accumulate) and/or evaluate the GGML **OpenCL/Adreno
backend** lineage for Mali. This is the ceiling on on-device prefill latency.

### 6. 🟡 User-downloaded (non-Eliza-1) models — apply every applicable optimization

Eliza-1 tiers ship pre-quantized (TurboQuant Q4 + QJL + Polar). Arbitrary
HuggingFace GGUFs the user installs should still get **every applicable** optimization:
quantize-if-unquantized (don't run F16/F32 weights when a quant works on the device),
KV-cache quantization, flash attention (where the backend's FA is correct), and MTP when
the model has a NextN/draft head. "Any model that can be quantized and isn't, should be."

---

## Optimization × tier × backend audit matrix (to fill in and keep green)

Per tier (`0_8b`, `2b`, `4b`, `9b`, `27b`, `27b-256k`) and backend
(CPU, Vulkan/Mali, Vulkan/desktop, Metal, CUDA), confirm each is **applied at runtime**
(not just present in the bundle):

- [ ] **TurboQuant** weights (turbo3/turbo4) — kernels actually dispatched at runtime
- [ ] **TCQ** (turbo3_tcq) on desktop/pro/server + long-ctx tiers
- [ ] **QJL** K-cache (and the fused QJL attention path, not the dequant hop)
- [ ] **PolarQuant / TBQ3** V-cache (and its fused path)
- [ ] **MTP** speculative decoding (2B+; wired through every host path incl. bionic)
- [ ] **Fused attention kernel** routed (no QJL→F16 dequant hop)
- [ ] **Flash attention** correct + enabled where the backend's FA is verified
- [ ] **KV-cache quantization** active on device (not silent F16 fallback)
- [ ] Weight-quant for user-installed models (no F16/F32 when a quant fits)

A cell is only ✅ when verified by the host harness and/or an on-device run with numbers.

---

## Proposed sequence

1. **Resolve the fused-op blockers** (#1): determine the shipped GGUF's Π (per-layer vs
   default), add causal masking to the fused op, and add a QJL-K+Polar-V op *or*
   standardize V on TBQ3_0. Validate bit-exact vs the host golden reference.
2. **Route the graph to the fused op** (#1) gated on `supports_op` + `cparams.flash_attn`
   so CPU/Vulkan/Metal/CUDA each pick fused-vs-generic correctly.
3. **Enable quantized KV on the bionic host** (#2) once #1 lands.
4. **Wire MTP through the bionic host** (#3).
5. **Fix or retire the generic Mali FA scalar race** (#4) — needed for non-fused KV
   types / non-Eliza-1 models; keep the `split_k=1` ARM gate + FA-disabled fallback until
   then.
6. **Mali matmul / prefill optimization** (#5).
7. **Generalize to user models** (#6): an "optimize on install" pass.

## Verification (no phone required for most of it)

- Host-side golden reference + dispatcher already exist for the fused/QJL/Polar/TBQ
  kernels: `plugins/plugin-local-inference/native/reference/` (`qjl_polar_ref.c`,
  `turbo_kernels.c`), fixtures via `gen_fixture.c`, and
  `native/verify/{vulkan_verify.cpp,metal_verify.mm,cuda_verify.cu}`. `vulkan_verify`
  runs on **lavapipe** (software Vulkan) on the Linux host.
- Add a host-side `flash_attn` split-K-vs-single-chunk correctness test (currently
  missing) to reproduce/guard the Mali race class.
- Record per-tier on-device numbers: prefill tok/s, decode tok/s, peak RSS, first-token
  latency, MTP acceptance rate — gate regressions in `eliza-1.manifest.json` `evals`.

## Notes / non-goals

- Upstream `ggml-org/llama.cpp` (fork base b10012, 2026-05-16) has **no Mali FA fix to
  backport** — the Mali correctness PRs (#23241/#22682/#23359) are still open; the
  post-base FA PRs (#24123/#23420) are AMD/coopmat-only. Our `split_k=1`-on-`VK_VENDOR_ID_ARM`
  gate is ahead of upstream — keep it; do not backport for FA correctness.
- The branding rule holds: user-facing strings say "Eliza-1", never the upstream/kernel
  names.

_Filed from an on-device optimization audit (Pixel 9a / Mali-G715). Kernel-level items
reference the `elizaOS/llama.cpp` submodule; runtime/wiring items are in this repo
(`plugins/plugin-local-inference`, `packages/app-core/platforms/android/.../elizavoice-jni`)._
18:T3871,## Summary

elizaOS already has strong, genuinely non-larp e2e machinery — the scenario-runner boots a real `AgentRuntime` over PGLite and exercises real plugin route handlers via its `api` turn kind, a deterministic LLM proxy with strict fixtures gives secret-free runs, a Mockoon-derived external-API mock fleet stands in for real provider calls, and a `external-api-live-drift.yml` nightly re-verifies those mocks against the real APIs. There are even static "ships-without-a-test → CI fails" gates for UI routes/pages and views (`route-coverage.test.ts`, `view-interaction-coverage.test.ts`, `plugin-tui-view-coverage.test.ts`). **What is missing is an umbrella coverage matrix and the equivalent ship-gate for the three surfaces that fall through the cracks: slash commands, pre-LLM shortcuts (#8791, new), and plugin-registered HTTP routes.** This issue is the audit/umbrella that inventories every command, shortcut, view, plugin and route, asserts each has a real recorded-mock e2e, and adds a CI gate that fails when a new one ships uncovered.

## Current state

### What is real and good (credit where due)

- **Scenario-runner is a real e2e harness, not larp.** `packages/scenario-runner/src/executor.ts` boots a real runtime and, for `kind: "api"` turns, stands up a loopback `http.createServer` that dispatches against `runtime.routes` (the actual plugin-registered handlers) and `fetch`es them (`startScenarioApiServer`, ~line 556; api-turn handler ~line 1261). `packages/scenario-runner/test/scenarios/deterministic-generated-app-routes.scenario.ts` is the gold standard: it registers a real `AppRegistryService`, calls real `handleAppsRoutes`/`handleAppPackageRoutes`/`handleViewsRoutes`, and asserts exact registry/catalog/route-ledger state — zero trivial assertions.
- **Secret-free determinism exists.** `packages/test/mocks/helpers/llm-proxy-plugin.ts` (`createDeterministicLlmProxyPlugin`, strict mode via `SCENARIO_LLM_PROXY_STRICT=1`) fails closed when no fixture matches, validates tool-call/schema output, and reports diagnostics. Wired through `runtime-factory.ts` (`shouldUseStrictDeterministicLlmProxy`).
- **Recorded provider mocks derived from real APIs, kept honest.** `packages/test/mocks/environments/*.json` (Mockoon) + the in-process stateful runner (`packages/test/mocks/scripts/start-mocks.ts`) cover ~24 providers; `packages/test/mocks/README.md` documents covered surfaces and remaining gaps; `provider-coverage-contract.test.ts` fails when the table drifts; `.github/workflows/external-api-live-drift.yml` re-runs `*.real.test.ts` against live APIs nightly to detect mock drift. This is exactly the "mock APIs derived from real calls, cached, validated in CI" the product owner asked for — for **external** APIs.
- **PR-lane deterministic e2e runs.** `.github/workflows/ci.yaml` runs `bun run --cwd packages/scenario-runner test:pr:e2e` (17 deterministic scenarios incl. `deterministic-generated-app-routes`, `deterministic-view-switching`, `deterministic-mcp-actions-routes`, `deterministic-workflow-actions-routes`, `deterministic-github-actions-routes`) plus a scenario-catalog completeness check (`packages/scripts/check-scenario-workflow-coverage.mjs`) that fails if any scenario in `packages/test/scenarios` is orphaned from the workflow globs. 701 scenario files exist.
- **Static ship-gates already exist for UI routes + views.** `packages/app/test/route-coverage.test.ts` diffs the canonical route catalog (`buildRouteCatalog`, mirroring `@elizaos/ui` `TAB_PATHS`) against the all-pages click-safe smoke matrix and fails when a new view/page/tile ships without smoke coverage. `packages/app/test/view-interaction-coverage.test.ts` and `packages/agent/src/__tests__/plugin-tui-view-coverage.test.ts` do the same for view interactions / TUI parity. Rich Playwright `ui-smoke` suite (~60 specs incl. `plugin-views-visual.spec.ts`, `orchestrator-gui-workbench.spec.ts`, `view-manager-actual-flow.spec.ts`).

### The gaps (the actual work)

1. **Slash commands: catalog is served, execution is never e2e-tested.** `plugins/plugin-commands` is a *registry only* — it ships `DEFAULT_COMMANDS` (~26 text commands) + a `COMMAND_REGISTRY` provider but **no action handlers** (confirmed in its CLAUDE.md and `src/index.ts`). The connector catalog (`src/connector-catalog.ts`) adds ~15 navigation/client commands. The wire surface is `GET /api/commands` (`packages/agent/src/api/commands-routes.ts`), consumed by `packages/ui/src/chat/useSlashCommandController.ts`. Tests today:
   - `packages/agent/src/api/commands-routes.test.ts` — calls the handler with **mocked** `json`/`error` fns (no real server, no execution).
   - `plugins/plugin-commands/__tests__/parser.test.ts` + `connector-catalog.test.ts` — unit-level; assert a handful of keys (`bash`, `think`), not per-command.
   - **No scenario, no Playwright spec types `/help` / `/settings` / `/model` into chat and asserts the command executes / navigates.** `grep` for any scenario referencing `api/commands` or slash execution returns empty. So most of the ~40 catalog commands have **zero** behavioral coverage of the click-to-effect path. (Pairs with #8790.)
2. **Shortcuts (pre-LLM action shortcuts): proposed, not built.** #8791 introduces a pre-LLM shortcut registry. It does not exist yet, so it has no coverage and no gate. This umbrella must include shortcuts in the matrix + gate the moment #8791 lands.
3. **Plugin HTTP routes: large route-level e2e gap.** ~38 plugins declare `Plugin.routes` (served in prod via `packages/agent/src/api/runtime-plugin-routes.ts` `tryHandleRuntimePluginRoute`; tested generically in `runtime-plugin-routes.test.ts`). Of the plugins that declare routes in their main index, these have **no dedicated route test and no `api`-turn scenario**: `plugin-anthropic-proxy`, `plugin-cli`, `plugin-computeruse`, `plugin-discord-local`, `plugin-facewear`, `plugin-farcaster`, `plugin-github`, `plugin-imessage`, `plugin-music`, `plugin-mysticism`, `plugin-telegram`, `plugin-video`, `plugin-workflow`, `plugin-xr`. (Covered: `plugin-bluebubbles`, `plugin-discord`, `plugin-signal`, `plugin-social-alpha`, `plugin-whatsapp` have `*-routes.test.ts`.) 10 plugins have **no test file at all** (`plugin-action-bench`, `plugin-discord-local`, `plugin-elizamaker`, `plugin-google-meet-cute`, `plugin-local-embedding`, `plugin-mlx`, `plugin-native-shared-types`, `plugin-omnivoice`, `plugin-tee`, `plugin-xmtp`).
4. **No coverage matrix and no ship-gate for commands/shortcuts/routes.** The existing gates cover UI routes/pages/views. There is **no** `check-command-coverage` / `check-shortcut-coverage` / `check-plugin-route-coverage` gate. The advisory `coverage-gate.yml` only measures line coverage on changed files (`COVERAGE_GATE_ENFORCE=0`) — it does not enforce that a new command/route/view has a behavioral e2e.

## Proposed work

Ordered; depends on the sibling "e2e mock infra" issue for any new recorded fixtures.

1. **Build the canonical coverage inventory (source of truth).** A boot-free script (mirror `packages/scripts/check-scenario-workflow-coverage.mjs` style) that enumerates, from real source:
   - slash commands — `DEFAULT_COMMANDS` (`plugins/plugin-commands/src/registry.ts`) ∪ `navigationCommands()`/client commands (`src/connector-catalog.ts`), i.e. the exact set `GET /api/commands` serves;
   - shortcuts — the #8791 registry (once it exists);
   - views — `Plugin.views` declarations resolved by `packages/agent/src/api/views-registry.ts` + `BUILTIN_VIEWS` (reuse the existing view catalog, do not duplicate #8796/#8797/#8798);
   - plugins + routes — every `Plugin.routes` entry across `plugins/*` (path + method).
   Emit `reports/coverage/e2e-matrix.json` + an HTML contact sheet (same shape as the scenario catalog inventory viewer).
2. **Close the command e2e gap (consumes #8790).** Add a `deterministic-slash-commands.scenario.ts` that hits `GET /api/commands` via an `api` turn and, for each `target.kind`, asserts the real effect: `agent` commands route through the message pipeline, `navigate` commands resolve to a real route, `client` commands fire the right effect. Add a Playwright `ui-smoke` spec that types representative slash commands into the composer and asserts navigation/clear/palette via `useSlashCommandController`. Every catalog command must be referenced by at least one of these.
3. **Close the plugin-route e2e gap.** For each route-declaring plugin currently uncovered (§3 list), add an `api`-turn scenario or a `*-routes.test.ts` that boots the handler through `tryHandleRuntimePluginRoute` (real dispatch, real auth gate, real body parsing) with recorded provider mocks where the route calls out. Prioritize plugins with public routes and user-facing surfaces.
4. **Wire shortcuts** into the matrix + a deterministic scenario as soon as #8791 lands.
5. **Add the CI ship-gate.** A `check-e2e-coverage` script + `e2e-coverage-gate.yml` (or a step in `ci.yaml`) that diffs the inventory (§1) against the discovered test/scenario matrix and **fails** when a command, shortcut, view, plugin, or route exists with no real e2e — following the exact precedent of `route-coverage.test.ts` ("a new view/page/tile that ships without smoke coverage fails CI here"). Start advisory for one cycle (like `coverage-gate.yml`), then flip to required once the baseline is green.
6. **Larp-detection pass.** Add a lint rule / review-checklist item so a "test" that asserts only shape (`length > 0`, `kind === 'navigate'`) without exercising the real handler does not count as coverage in the gate — the gate should require a real `api`/`action`/Playwright turn, not a mocked-`json`-fn unit test.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] A committed coverage inventory (`reports/coverage/e2e-matrix.json` + viewer) enumerating every slash command, shortcut, view, plugin, and route from real source.
- [ ] Every command in the `GET /api/commands` catalog has a real e2e (scenario `api`/`action` turn or Playwright spec) that exercises its actual effect — not a mocked handler unit test.
- [ ] Every shortcut in the #8791 registry has a deterministic e2e (gated on #8791 landing; tracked as a checklist item until then).
- [ ] Every route-declaring plugin in the §3 uncovered list has a route-level e2e booting the real handler via `tryHandleRuntimePluginRoute`/`api`-turn, with recorded provider mocks for any external calls.
- [ ] All 10 zero-test plugins either gain a real test or are documented as intentionally test-exempt with written justification.
- [ ] Views remain covered by the existing gates (no regression); this issue references #8796/#8797/#8798 rather than re-implementing view e2e.
- [ ] A `check-e2e-coverage` CI gate fails when a new command/shortcut/view/plugin/route ships without a real e2e; advisory for one cycle, then required.
- [ ] New behavioral tests run deterministically in the PR lane (no secrets) via `SCENARIO_USE_LLM_PROXY=1` + the Mockoon mock fleet; any new external fixtures are covered by `external-api-live-drift.yml`.
- [ ] `bun run --cwd packages/scenario-runner test:pr:e2e` and the new gate are green on `develop`.

## Files & areas likely involved

- `plugins/plugin-commands/src/registry.ts`, `src/connector-catalog.ts`, `src/parser.ts` — command source of truth.
- `packages/agent/src/api/commands-routes.ts` + `commands-routes.test.ts` — `GET /api/commands` (currently mocked-only).
- `packages/ui/src/chat/useSlashCommandController.ts`, `slash-menu.ts`, `components/shell/SlashCommandMenu.tsx` — command execution consumers.
- `packages/agent/src/api/runtime-plugin-routes.ts` (`tryHandleRuntimePluginRoute`) + `runtime-plugin-routes.test.ts` — real plugin-route dispatch.
- `packages/agent/src/api/views-registry.ts`, `views-routes.ts`, `builtin-views.ts` — view surface (reference, don't dup).
- `packages/scenario-runner/src/executor.ts` (`startScenarioApiServer`, `api`-turn handler), `runtime-factory.ts`, `test/scenarios/deterministic-generated-app-routes.scenario.ts` (template).
- `packages/test/mocks/` — `helpers/llm-proxy-plugin.ts`, `environments/*.json`, `scripts/start-mocks.ts`, `helpers/provider-coverage.ts`, `README.md`.
- Existing gate precedents to extend: `packages/app/test/route-coverage.test.ts`, `view-interaction-coverage.test.ts`, `ui-smoke-coverage.test.ts`, `packages/agent/src/__tests__/plugin-tui-view-coverage.test.ts`, `packages/scripts/check-scenario-workflow-coverage.mjs`.
- CI: `.github/workflows/ci.yaml`, `scenario-pr.yml`, `test.yml`, `coverage-gate.yml`, `external-api-live-drift.yml`, `live-scenarios.yml`; new `check-e2e-coverage` script under `packages/scripts/`.
- Uncovered route plugins (new tests): `plugin-anthropic-proxy`, `plugin-cli`, `plugin-computeruse`, `plugin-discord-local`, `plugin-facewear`, `plugin-farcaster`, `plugin-github`, `plugin-imessage`, `plugin-music`, `plugin-mysticism`, `plugin-telegram`, `plugin-video`, `plugin-workflow`, `plugin-xr`.

## Open questions

- **Gate scope per surface:** should the gate require coverage of *every* route on a plugin, or one representative happy-path + auth-gate per plugin? (Per-route is exhaustive but high-churn; per-plugin-surface is the cheaper floor.)
- **What counts as "covered" for a command?** One scenario per `target.kind` (agent/navigate/client) vs one per command. Recommend per-command for `agent`-target commands (behavioral) and per-kind for navigate/client (effect is structural).
- **Larp threshold:** do we hard-fail the gate on mocked-`json`-fn unit tests (like the current `commands-routes.test.ts`), or just not count them? Recommend "don't count, don't fail" so existing unit tests stay.
- **Connector command parity:** should Discord/Telegram native-command registration (`plugins/plugin-discord/catalog-commands.ts`, `plugins/plugin-telegram/src/command-registration.ts`) be in-scope for this gate, or owned by #8790? (Currently they have catalog unit tests but no live-render e2e.)
- **Provider-key-gated routes:** for plugins whose routes only work with real credentials and have no mock yet (e.g. some `plugin-farcaster`/`plugin-video` paths), do we require a new Mockoon fixture (blocks on the mock-infra issue) or accept a `external-api-live-drift`-style nightly-only real test?
- **#8791 sequencing:** shortcuts can't be gated until the registry exists — confirm this umbrella stays open across #8791, or splits the shortcut requirement into a follow-up.
19:T1b21,## Background

LifeOps was split from the old mega-plugin surface into `plugin-personal-assistant` plus domain plugins for calendar, scheduling/reminders, goals, inbox, health, blocker/focus, finances, documents, phone/messages, relationships, etc.

A deep local audit found the current static/code-level split is in good shape: stale registered LifeOps action surfaces were removed, personal-assistant owner actions have role gates plus handler-level owner checks, the split plugins build/test in isolation, and the shared scheduled-task architecture is still routed through the personal-assistant runner rather than a second LifeOps scheduler.

The remaining work is not more local grep/testing. It is live, account-backed validation across OWNER and AGENT sides, native devices, and OAuth/provider state.

## Already validated locally

- `plugin-personal-assistant` focused tests/build passed.
- Split plugin focused tests/typechecks passed for relationships, documents, phone, health, goals, calendar, blocker, scheduling, reminders, finances, inbox, and todos.
- Root `bun run verify` passed before the final upstream submodule-only merge.
- Final upstream sync merged latest `origin/develop`; `origin/develop` is an ancestor of the working branch.
- Final submodule-specific validation passed for `plugins/plugin-local-inference` after the upstream `llama.cpp` gitlink change.
- No stale `plugin-lifeops` migration markers were found by static scan.
- Core planned-tool execution and message action filtering both enforce `roleGate`.
- Personal-assistant owner actions now also guard at handler execution time.

## Remaining work

### 1. Provision live test identities

Create and document the actual test identities/devices needed to validate the split end to end:

- OWNER Google account with Calendar and Gmail enabled.
- AGENT Google account or grant state where the agent has non-owner identity.
- OWNER and AGENT identities for Telegram, Discord, Signal, WhatsApp, iMessage, X, and Slack if Slack is part of the deployed LifeOps surface.
- Twilio test number and recipient allowlist for phone/voice flows.
- Native devices or simulators for iOS/macOS/Android flows that cannot be proven in unit tests.
- Health-provider accounts/devices for Apple Health/HealthKit, Google Fit or Health Connect, Fitbit, Oura, Strava, and Withings as applicable.
- Finance/subscription data sources: Gmail billing corpus, CSV fixture, Plaid or equivalent sandbox, PayPal sandbox if supported.
- Browser/password-manager/remote-desktop/travel-provider accounts where those connectors are expected to participate in personal assistant workflows.

### 2. Validate OWNER vs AGENT permission matrix

For every connector/action surface, test these states and record evidence:

- Unauthenticated connector.
- OWNER authenticated and authorized.
- AGENT authenticated but not owner-authorized.
- Expired/revoked grant.
- Missing required scope.
- Multiple grants where owner-side selection must win.
- Planned tool execution path.
- Direct handler invocation path.
- UI-triggered path from the relevant view.

Expected behavior:

- Owner-only actions deny non-owner execution with a clear permission error.
- Handler-level guards match `roleGate` behavior.
- Connector calls return typed `DispatchResult` data, not booleans or swallowed failures.
- Approval-required outbound actions route through the approval policy rather than sending silently.
- Owner-side grants are selected for owner-only operations.

### 3. Validate live connectors

Run live send/read/sync smoke tests for:

- Google Calendar: list, create/update/delete or draft event, availability, scheduling conflict handling, owner-vs-agent grant behavior.
- Gmail/inbox: read/search, label/archive where supported, outbound email approval flow, billing/subscription extraction if used by finances.
- Telegram, Discord, Signal, WhatsApp, iMessage, X, Slack: status/read/send or draft/send paths, unavailable states, revoked auth, and owner-vs-agent behavior.
- Phone/voice/SMS: Twilio env, outbound call/SMS authorization, inbound routing if supported, Android SMS default-role/native bridge behavior where applicable.
- Health: Apple Health/HealthKit permissions, Google Fit/Health Connect permissions, Fitbit/Oura/Strava/Withings sync and unavailable/error paths.
- Blocker/focus: macOS hosts/SelfControl/admin permission flows, iOS Family Controls, Android Usage Access.
- Finances: subscription detection, payment/provider import, CSV/sandbox paths, approval boundaries for sensitive operations.
- Documents/browser/password-manager/remote desktop/travel providers if included in the deployed assistant workflows.

### 4. Validate views and integration flows

Verify each split view renders, navigates, refreshes, and performs its expected actions with both mock and live data:

- Calendar.
- Scheduling/reminders.
- Goals.
- Inbox.
- Health.
- Blocker/focus.
- Finances/subscriptions.
- Documents.
- Relationships.
- Phone/messages.
- Any remaining compatibility `#lifeops` deep links or aliases in `packages/app`.

For each view, capture:

- Empty state.
- Loading state.
- Error/unavailable connector state.
- Populated state.
- Refresh/retry behavior.
- Mobile and desktop layout.
- Navigation from app launcher/sidebar/deep link.
- Agent-created data showing up in the view.
- View action resulting in the correct agent/tool/connector behavior.

### 5. Add durable live QA artifacts

After accounts/devices are available, add or update:

- A documented LifeOps live-validation matrix with account/device prerequisites.
- Repeatable smoke-test instructions for local and CI-adjacent validation.
- Redacted screenshots or logs proving each connector/view state was exercised.
- Any missing scenario-runner or e2e coverage that can be safely automated without real secrets.
- Clear skip behavior for live tests when credentials/devices are absent.

## Acceptance criteria

- All listed connector families have OWNER and AGENT side evidence.
- Every owner-only action has matching denial behavior through planned tools, direct handlers, and UI-triggered flows.
- Every split view has been checked on desktop and mobile with empty, loading, error, and populated states.
- OAuth/native permission failures are explicit and recoverable in the UI and agent responses.
- Live connector failures return typed results and do not silently succeed.
- Approval-gated outbound flows are validated end to end.
- The validation matrix documents exactly which accounts, devices, scopes, env vars, and provider sandboxes were used.
- Any discovered bug gets a linked issue or PR before this issue is closed.

## Notes

The local audit did not identify remaining stale LifeOps cruft in registered plugin surfaces. The known gap is confidence that real-world OAuth, native permissions, side selection, provider-specific failure modes, and UI integration all behave correctly with live accounts and devices.

1b:Taed,Moves the first-party curated registry out of `app-core` into **`@elizaos/registry/first-party`** and flips entry ownership to **plugin-side** with build-time aggregation. (#9177, the game-app removal this was stacked on, is now **merged**; this PR is auto-retargeted to `develop`.) Three commits:

### 1 — extract the package + shim + runtime overlay
- Moved `schema.ts` / `loader.ts` / `index.ts` / `app-registry.ts` out of `packages/app-core/src/registry/` into `packages/registry/src/first-party/`, exported as **`@elizaos/registry/first-party`**.
- `packages/app-core/src/registry/index.ts` is now a **one-line re-export shim**, so `@elizaos/app-core/registry` + the app-core barrel stay stable — the sole cross-package consumer (`plugin-registry`), the capacitor-bridge tsconfig path, and the hyperliquid vitest alias need **no change**.
- Added **`registerRegistryEntry()`** — a Symbol-keyed global overlay so any plugin can contribute/override an entry **at runtime** (validated fail-loud; runtime wins by `id`).
- Preserved the boot-critical invariants verbatim (`var cacheSlot` TDZ-hardening, circular-import self-heal, on-device `process.argv[1]` fallback). Moved the 3 registry tests with the code.

### 2 — plugin-owned entries + aggregation
- Relocated all **110 entries**: 77 → their owning plugin/package dir as `registry-entry.json`; 33 → `first-party/curated/` (8 built-in app-viewers, 23 entries for non-vendored plugins, 2 no-npmName). Deleted the central `entries/` dir.
- Added **`generate.ts`** — scans `plugins/*` + `packages/*` `registry-entry.json` (object or array) + `curated/`, validates fail-loud, dedupes by `id`, writes a single committed **`generated.json`**. Scripts: `generate:first-party` + `generate:first-party:check` (CI drift gate).
- Loader now reads the **single `generated.json`** (vs scanning per-kind dirs) → on-device staging is one file, mirroring the community registry pattern.

### 3 — plugins ship their entry on publish
- Added `registry-entry.json` to the `files` array of all 77 plugins/packages that own one, so a published plugin carries its own registry metadata for downstream aggregation (the extensible, plugin-side path).

### Verification
- `loadRegistry()` output **BYTE-IDENTICAL** to the pre-refactor golden — after the package move *and* the plugin-side migration: **110 entries** (18 app / 68 plugin / 24 connector), identical ids/npmNames/groups.
- Plugin-side overlay proven (register → 110→111; override-by-id keeps 111 with the new value); shim resolves end-to-end.
- Typecheck clean (`@elizaos/registry`, `app-core`, `plugin-registry`); registry tests green (23); `generated.json` drift-check green; smartglasses gate self-test green.

20:Tc6a,## What this is

**Wave 0 of the coding-capability test-harness initiative** — a thorough audit of elizaOS's code-writing + agent-orchestration capability and its test/evidence coverage, plus the two critical correctness fixes and the first batch of additive tests that audit surfaced.

The audit (12 domains, 85 gaps: 8 critical / 33 high / 35 medium / 9 low) is committed under `packages/scenario-runner/docs/coding-capability-audit/` (`MASTER_PLAN.md`, `findings.json`, `PROGRESS.md`) as the actionable backlog for the remaining waves.

## Changes (each verified)

**Correctness fixes (2 critical bugs):**
- **Per-session workspace isolation** (`#audit-0.2`) — `resolveDefaultSpawnWorkdir` collapsed every route-less concurrent task into ONE shared directory, so simultaneous coding projects corrupted each other's files. Now a shared scratch root is flagged `isolate` and `spawnSession` gives each session its own `<root>/task-<id>` subdir (`computeSessionWorkdir`); cwd self-checkout and route/explicit dirs are untouched.
- **Two-phase reload rollback** (`#audit-0.3`) — `applyPluginRuntimeMutation` could unload plugins then fail mid-register, leaving the runtime half-torn-down. Now `rollbackPartialReload` restores the previous plugin graph before any restart fallback.

**Harness repair + drift guard:**
- **task-agent live E2E repaired** (`#audit-0.4`) — fixed a 3-axis breakage (missing smoke-script path, a `counter-app` mode that never existed, removed `PTYService`/`cleanForChat` imports) + a new non-live `live-smoke-imports.test.ts` that catches this class of drift in CI.
- **`@elizaos/shared` vitest resolution** (`#audit-0.1`) — the `plugin-task-coordinator` suite failed to load (`@elizaos/shared/voice-eot`), so its 18-test TaskInspector suite never ran. Fixed via source aliases.

**Additive unit coverage (6 new tests):** model-chooser contract, account-usage quota-% parsing, notify-on-completion, spawn-gate, file-lock contention, progress cadence.

24:Te26,# Relates to

New plugin. Opens discussion around ERC-8183 as a lifecycle standard for agent-to-agent commerce in elizaOS.

# Sync with develop

- [x] Rebased onto latest `origin/develop`
- [x] `bun run verify` passes

Within `packages/plugin-agent-commerce-pqc`:

$ bun run verify

✓ TypeScript type-check passes
✓ Vitest suite passes

35 tests passed.

# Risks

Medium.

This PR adds a new opt-in plugin under `packages/plugin-agent-commerce-pqc`. No existing runtime behavior is modified.

The plugin registers:

- 1 Service (`agent_commerce`)
- 4 Actions
- 1 Provider

State is persisted through the existing `runtime.createMemory()` API using the `erc8183:job:` namespace.

Known operational risks:

- hydration currently loads up to 1000 job records per agent
- rate limiting is process-local and not distributed
- reputation persistence is memory-backed by default

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Adds an ERC-8183-inspired commerce workflow for elizaOS agents.

The plugin enables one agent to:

1. Create a job
2. Fund a job
3. Submit a deliverable
4. Evaluate the result
5. Complete, refund, expire, or abort the workflow

State machine:

OPEN → FUNDED → SUBMITTED → COMPLETE
                           ↘ REFUND
              ↘ EXPIRED
         ↘ ABORTED

## Registered extension points

| Type | Name |
|--------|--------|
| Service | agent_commerce |
| Action | CREATE_SECURE_JOB |
| Action | FUND_SECURE_JOB |
| Action | SUBMIT_DELIVERABLE |
| Action | JOB_STATUS |
| Provider | COMMERCE_CONTEXT |

## Persistence

Jobs are stored through `runtime.createMemory()` using:

erc8183:job:<jobId>

Each state transition appends a new memory record.

On restart, the store lazily hydrates from memory records and reconstructs the latest job state using the highest `updatedAt` value.

## Security features

- SHA-256 payload attestation
- Deliverable hash verification
- Reputation-based funding gate
- Rate limiting
- State transition validation
- Idempotent terminal transitions

## What kind of change is this?

Feature (non-breaking).

All changes are additive.

# Documentation changes needed?

No.

The package includes its own README.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

- `src/elizaos/index.ts`
- `src/service.ts`
- `src/state-machine.ts`
- `src/store/elizaos.ts`

## Issue status

Related to #8887 and #8912. Both were closed on `develop` while this branch was being verified; this PR is follow-up hardening/evidence on top of that landed work, so it intentionally avoids auto-close keywords.2a:T7dc,## What

A model-provider plugin (`@elizaos/plugin-cli-inference`) that serves chat/planner inference by spawning the **local Claude Code / Codex CLI** (`claude --print` / `codex exec`) as eliza model handlers, instead of calling the vendor HTTP API.

## Why

This lets an agent run its chat on the operator's **Claude Max / ChatGPT subscription via the sanctioned CLI** — the licensed vehicle for those subscriptions — rather than replaying a consumer-subscription token against the API. Useful for self-hosted operators and for per-user dedicated cloud containers where each user brings their own CLI auth.

## Design

- **Inert unless `ELIZA_CHAT_VIA_CLI=claude|codex`** — self-gated; loads via `PROVIDER_PLUGIN_MAP` + its own `autoEnableModule`. Zero effect on existing paths when unset.
- Registers `TEXT_LARGE` / `TEXT_MEGA` / `RESPONSE_HANDLER` at **priority 100** so it deterministically wins those tiers when enabled. **Does not** register `ACTION_PLANNER` — the CLI can't honor GBNF / native-tool / `responseSchema` constrained decoding, so the planner stays on the configured grammar/tool-honoring provider. `RESPONSE_HANDLER` (the user-facing reply) is the intended sub-served tier.
- Threads eliza's **full composed prompt** (`system` + `messages`) to the CLI, so the model produces eliza's exact structured output.
- **Sandboxed**: runs in an isolated empty cwd, env-filtered (`filterEnv`), binary-allowlisted; never injects the subscription token into the child env or logs (the CLI reads `~/.claude` / `~/.codex` itself). stderr is value-redacted.
- Per-call timeout with SIGKILL escalation + process-group kill; throws on timeout/non-zero-exit/empty-output so the runtime falls back to the configured provider.

## Testing

Unit tests (26) mock the spawn and assert argv, context threading, the timeout/error paths, and binary resolution. Verified end-to-end against the real `claude --print` and `codex exec` binaries.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)
2b:T9c2,Part of EPIC #9105 (Computer Use × Vision). This enables and verifies the
**Android on-device screen-understanding loop end-to-end on a Pixel 9a**:
capture the screen with MediaProjection → describe it on the GPU with a local
vision model — no cloud, no ONNX.

## What this does
- **plugin-vision is mobile-loadable**: lazy `sharp` (pure-JS jimp fallback) +
  lazy native YOLO/face detectors, so the module evaluates on Android; it's
  bundled into the mobile agent bundle (literal `import()`), loaded `blocking`
  so its routes register at boot.
- **Agent-triggered screen-capture bridge**: the action requests a frame, the
  renderer (native MediaProjection) captures + submits it back
  (`/api/vision/capture-requests` + `/api/vision/screen-frame`).
- **Native jpeg+scale capture + warm MediaProjection reuse** for battery/latency
  (the renderer asks for `{format:"jpeg",scale:0.5,quality:75}` so resize/encode
  happen natively, and the projection + VirtualDisplay stay warm across frames).
- **Android-14 MediaProjection foreground service**: a `mediaProjection` FGS is
  started before `getMediaProjection()` (API 34 throws `SecurityException`
  otherwise). This was the root blocker for capture.
- **On-device IMAGE_DESCRIPTION**: routed to the bionic GPU host `op:"image"`
  (`eliza-1-2b` + `vision/mmproj-2b.gguf`), with a repetition-collapse so the
  small model's output is bounded/low-token.
- **Crash fix**: the bionic host shared one resident llama context between
  text-generate and vision-describe; prefilling text after a vision (mtmd) eval
  aborted the process (SIGABRT). Now the context is rebuilt only on a
  text↔vision switch, so continuous chat and continuous describe stay warm.

## Verified on Pixel 9a (53081JEBF11586)
- Capture: `captureScreenshot(...)` → `{ok:true,w:540,h:1212,len:15280}`, a real
  540×1212 JPEG of the live home screen; VirtualDisplay
  `ScreenCapture(virtual:ai.elizaos.app)` created with **no SecurityException**.
- Describe: that exact frame → on-device GPU →
  *"The screen shows an orange background with a text input field at the bottom.
  The text input field has a plus icon on the left and a microphone icon on the
  right."* (accurate — the Eliza composer).
- plugin-vision route `GET /api/vision/capture-requests` → 200; renderer bridge
  polling confirmed in logcat.

Evidence: `.github/issue-evidence/9105-android-capture-describe.md` (+ screenshot).

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)2c:T8df,Implements the Tier-1 deliverable of #9478.

## What
Adds `plugins/plugin-app-control/test/scenarios/views-crud-lifecycle.scenario.ts` — the **first scenario that exercises all three owner-authoring sub-modes** of the unified `VIEWS` action (`create`, `edit`, `delete`) in one live-planner run. Existing view scenarios (`views-list`, `views-show`, `views-search`, `views-voice-navigate`) are read/navigate-only.

3 turns → 3 assertions + an honest judge:
- `selectedAction: VIEWS` and `selectedActionArguments includesAny [create|edit|delete|remove]`
- `actionCalled VIEWS minCount: 3`
- a `judgeRubric` requiring the assistant to acknowledge scaffold/dispatch (create), edit dispatch (edit), and confirm-or-not-found (delete), and to **never falsely claim** a view was created, edited, or deleted.

## Why this shape (and what it deliberately does NOT fake)
`VIEWS` create/edit gate on owner access and then **dispatch a coding sub-agent via `START_CODING_TASK`**, which is *not* registered in the scenario runtime (`packages/scenario-runner/src/runtime-factory.ts`) — so a created view never actually registers, and `delete` has no non-protected view to remove. Asserting the **catalog-membership delta** (a view appearing in / disappearing from `GET /api/views`) therefore requires a test-only synthetic-view registration seam in `packages/agent/src/api/views-registry.ts`. That **Tier-2** enhancement is documented inline and in #9478; this PR is the honest routing/dispatch floor it will build on — it does not claim an effect it can't observe.

## Verification
- Modeled field-for-field on the proven `app-create-no-existing` / `app-create-cancel` scenarios in the same directory (same `lane: "live-only"`, `requires`, `finalChecks` shapes).
- Routing dependency confirmed: `VIEWS.validate` for create/edit/delete requires only `ownerCheck` (not view existence) — the **same** `defaultOwnerAccessFn` gate `APP` create uses, whose scenarios already pass in the live lane (`plugins/plugin-app-control/src/actions/views.ts`, `app.ts:145`).
- No coverage-test breakage: `deterministic-action-coverage.test.ts` scans only `packages/scenario-runner/test/scenarios`, not the plugin scenario dir.

2e:Tc35,Builds on #8883 (merged). Closes #8896, #8897, #8894. Part of #8884 (grilling epic).

Three commits that complete the core of the "grill sub-agents until truly done, with real proof" loop:

### #8896 — auto-generate default acceptance criteria so verification *always* fires
`autoVerifyCompletion` previously fast-pathed to pass when a task had no `acceptanceCriteria` — the common case ("fix this bug"), so most tasks skipped grilling entirely. Now `OrchestratorTaskService.createTask` (the single choke point for the action, the route, and `forkTask`) mints 3-5 measurable criteria from a task-type template (`coding` / `app-build` / `view-create` / `deploy`), optionally refined by a small model (fully defensive — falls back to the static template, never throws). Gated by `ELIZA_REQUIRE_GOAL_CONTRACT` (default on); caller-supplied criteria are never overwritten.

### #8897 — escalating socratic grill protocol with evidence checklist
The corrective follow-up to a failing sub-agent now escalates by attempt number: **(1)** collegial proof request → **(2)** pointed socratic questions citing the prior failure → **(3)** final last-chance before human escalation. Every correction appends a checkbox evidence checklist (one item per unmet criterion, each carrying its `proofDemandFor` demand) the worker must return ticked with the artifact inline. The call site forwards the persisted `autoVerifyAttempts` counter.

### #8894 — typed CompletionEvidenceBundle with tool-output + trajectory persistence
Formalizes the evidence the verifier sees as a typed `CompletionEvidenceBundle` (summary, diff summary, `{test,build,lint,raw}` tool output, verified URLs, screenshots, trajectory path). `collectEvidenceBundle` mines the real git change set, extracts test/build/lint stdout from recorded `tool_running`/`tool_result` events, and persists the bundle as JSONL (path recorded on a `completion_evidence_persisted` event) — all off the critical path, IO failures swallowed.

32:T1348,## LifeOps decomposition — Slice 4: promote approval-queue to `@elizaos/agent` `ApprovalService`

Implements **Slice 4** of the LifeOps extraction plan: `plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/docs/lifeops-extraction-plan.md` → "Slice 4 — Promote approval-queue to `@elizaos/agent` `ApprovalService`".

Follows the just-merged **Slice 3** precedent (`pending-prompts` / `global-pause` / `handoff` runtime services, #9197) and the `KnowledgeGraphService` template.

Part of #8833.

### Why no data risk
`approval_requests` is owned by **`@elizaos/plugin-sql`** (`plugins/plugin-sql/src/schema/approvalRequests.ts`, public schema — no schema prefix). This is a pure runtime-service promotion of PA's `lifeops/approval-queue.ts` ops. **No data migration.**

### What moved

New `packages/agent/src/services/approval/`:

| File | Contents |
|---|---|
| `store.ts` | The **copied** `PgApprovalQueue` ops from PA `lifeops/approval-queue.ts` — `ALLOWED_TRANSITIONS` + `enqueue`/`list`/`byId`/`approve`/`reject`/`markExecuting`/`markDone`/`markExpired`/`purgeExpired`. Raw SQL kept **identical** (no schema prefix → targets plugin-sql's public `approval_requests`). |
| `sql.ts` | Self-contained raw-SQL helpers (mirrors `knowledge-graph/sql.ts`) — `executeRawSql` / `parseJsonRecord` / `sqlText` / `sqlInteger` / `sqlJson` / `toText`. |
| `types.ts` | Agent-private approval transport types. The `book_travel` payload's travel sub-shapes are declared **structurally** (no Duffel/PA dependency) — the runtime state machine + SQL + validation only need the structural shape, so the runtime carries no plugin dependency (inward-only law). |
| `service.ts` | `class ApprovalService extends Service` — serviceType literal `'eliza_approval'`, `getQueue(agentId)` accessor, `resolveApprovalService(runtime)`. |
| `index.ts` | Barrel. |
| `service.test.ts` | 8 unit tests over an in-memory `approval_requests` fake (state-machine happy/reject/purge, `agentId` scoping, `ApprovalNotFoundError` / `ApprovalStateTransitionError`). |

Wiring:
- Registered `ApprovalService` in `createElizaPlugin()` `services[]` next to the Slice-3 services.
- Named re-export from `packages/agent/src/index.ts` (KG / Slice-3 pattern — keeps it out of the broad services barrel to avoid TS2308).

PA delegation (`plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/src/lifeops/approval-queue.ts`):
- `createApprovalQueue(runtime, { agentId })` now resolves the runtime `ApprovalService` when present (first-wins dedup), **falling back to the local `PgApprovalQueue` when absent** — matches the Slice-3 delegation pattern.
- PA keeps its precise travel/Duffel-typed `approval-queue.types.ts` and its local impl (the fallback). All 6 PA approval-type consumers are unchanged. All runtime string literals (serviceType, action/event names) preserved.

### Architecture notes
- Inward-only law holds: `@elizaos/agent` does **not** import PA (the approval types are declared agent-private; the `book_travel` `search`/`passengers`/`calendarSync` shapes are inlined structurally rather than importing PA's Duffel-coupled `TravelBookingPayloadFields`).
- `'eliza_approval'` is a brand-new, unique serviceType (no collision — verified repo-wide).

33:Te77,## What & why

Closes the remaining measurement + re-render/repaint gaps of #9141 ("drive the
dashboard shell to a sustained 60/120fps"). The frame-budget *math* had already
landed — three times, in parallel — so this PR makes it **one** primitive,
**measures** the 60/120fps target end-to-end, and removes the last whole-store
subscriptions.

## Changes

1. **Consolidate 3 duplicate frame-budget primitives → 1.** `hooks/frame-budget.ts`,
   `perf/frame-budget.ts`, and `hooks/useFrameBudget.ts` had landed independently
   with **three** stat shapes, **two** event channels (one of which the issue
   explicitly forbids), and three jank definitions. Now a single
   `hooks/frame-budget.ts` (`summarizeFrameSamples` + a `FrameBudgetSampler` that
   owns the rAF loop + longtask observer) backs the dev `PerfOverlay`, the
   telemetry monitor (on the shared `RENDER_TELEMETRY_EVENT` channel), and the new
   KPI spec — one fps/jank/long-task math everywhere. The second-channel
   `FrameBudgetMeter` (no real consumers) is deleted.

2. **Interaction-framerate KPI spec** (`packages/app/test/ui-smoke/perf-interaction-kpi.spec.ts`
   + reusable `lib/frame-kpi.ts`) — the measurement the issue said we were flying
   blind on. An in-page rAF sampler records inter-frame deltas during real
   interactions and summarizes them with the same math as the live HUD.

3. **Reduced-motion collapse spec** (`perf-reduced-motion.spec.ts`) — forces
   `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` and proves both the framer-motion media query
   and the `motion-reduce:animate-none` CSS contract collapse, with no shell
   element left animating. Also fixes the one unguarded shell animation — the
   "Copied" label fade now honors `reduce`.

4. **Finish the `useApp()` → selector migration + lock it.** Converts
   `useShellController` (the hottest shell controller — was still on the whole
   monolithic store) to a 14-field `useAppSelectorShallow`; adds
   `useapp-selector-gate.test.ts` that fails the build on any new `useApp()` call
   site. (The two smaller hooks were converted on develop in parallel.)

34:Tb31,Part of #8876 (unified attachments & files, v1). **PR0 — security + quick wins**, fully additive on top of the existing content-addressed media store. No new DB table, no `File` entity, no refcount/GC engine; `ContentType` stays frozen.

## What this does
**Serve-path stored-XSS hardening** (`packages/agent/src/api/media-store.ts`)
- SVG + unknown/active MIME types are served `Content-Disposition: attachment` (never inline-rendered); images/audio/video/pdf stay inline so `<img>`/`<video>` keep working.
- `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` + a sandboxed CSP on **every** serve response — both the HTTP path and the in-process iOS route path.
- Magic-byte reconciliation: bytes that sniff as SVG/HTML but are declared `image/*` are stored + served as their **true** (download-only) type.

**Client URL scheme allowlist** (`packages/ui/src/utils/attachment-url.ts`)
- `isSafeAttachmentUrl` enforced before rendering any agent-provided attachment URL in `MessageAttachments`. Rejects `javascript:`, `data:text/html`, `data:image/svg+xml`, `file:`, scheme-relative, malformed, etc.

**Download + share** (`packages/ui/src/utils/download-share.ts`)
- Transport-capability helper (web `showSaveFilePicker`/`<a download>` + `navigator.share`; native via the Capacitor **global bridge** — no new dependency added). Wired into `MediaGalleryView` and `documents-detail`.

**Client size validation** (`packages/ui/src/utils/image-attachment.ts`)
- Per-file (20MB) + total (60MB) caps; oversized files are dropped **before** upload instead of silently sliced, with a "kept N, dropped M (too large)" notice surfaced in `ChatView` (action notice) and `ContinuousChatOverlay` (inline).

**Cleanup + docs**
- Removed verified-dead `blob.ts` `ensureElizaCloudUrl`/`isFalAiUrl` and documents `fetchUrlContent` + no-op `logCacheMetrics`.
- Added the "one content-addressed store / additive model" anti-pattern clause to root `CLAUDE.md` == `AGENTS.md`.

## Tests / verification
- New tests: serve-security headers + markup-sniff (agent `media-store.test.ts`), `attachment-url` allowlist (25), `download-share` (17), `image-attachment` size-partition (20).
- `typecheck` + Biome green across **agent / ui / core / cloud-shared**; agent media tests (32), core documents tests (22) pass.

## Acceptance criteria covered (from #8876)
- ✅ Serve returns `Content-Disposition: attachment` for SVG/active types + `nosniff` + CSP (asserted by test); an SVG with embedded script cannot execute on the dashboard origin.
- ✅ Client per-file size validation + "kept N dropped M" notice.
- ✅ Download/share helper present (web/desktop verified; native via Capacitor global bridge — full native save verified in the later native PR).
- ✅ No new DB table / File entity / refcount-GC / enum unification introduced.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)35:Td6a,Closes #9567.

## What
A new standalone **`@elizaos/plugin-embeddings`** — a provider-agnostic ("bring your own") `TEXT_EMBEDDING` provider. Point one set of `EMBEDDING_*` vars at **any** OpenAI-compatible `/embeddings` endpoint (personal OpenAI key, Eliza Cloud, Voyage, Together, local TEI/Infinity/vLLM/LM Studio) and embeddings work **independent of the chat brain**.

## Why
Embeddings were coupled to the chat provider. A self-hosted bot on **Claude** (no embeddings API) or **Cerebras** (no `/embeddings`) had no clean path to real embeddings — `plugin-elizacloud` bundles `TEXT_EMBEDDING` with priority-50 text handlers that hijack the chat brain and is gated to a cloud `eliza_xxx` key; `plugin-openai`'s `OPENAI_EMBEDDING_*` override only works when plugin-openai is itself enabled and smuggles non-OpenAI providers under an `OPENAI_` namespace. This generalizes the existing `plugin-lmstudio` (`capabilities:["embedding"]`) pattern into a chat-agnostic one.

## Design (purely additive, zero regression)
- Registers **only** `TEXT_EMBEDDING` + `TEXT_EMBEDDING_BATCH` — no text/image/audio handlers, no actions/providers/services.
- **Load-gated**: auto-enables only when `EMBEDDING_BASE_URL` or `EMBEDDING_API_KEY` is set → not loaded on any existing deployment.
- **Priority `1`**: `local-inference @0 < plugin-embeddings @1 < Eliza Cloud @50` — opt-in beats a bare local embedder, yields to a paired Cloud. (This is the one product call from #9567 — happy to change the band.)
- **Throws on every real failure** (issue #9324 / Commandment 8) — never a zero/garbage vector. Null boot-probe returns a correctly-sized marker for the dimension probe only.
- Raw `fetch` (no `@ai-sdk` dep); `@elizaos/core` peer only. Browser build suppresses the `Authorization` header unless `EMBEDDING_BROWSER_URL` (server proxy) is set.
- **Zero changes** to core / plugin-sql / plugin-openai / plugin-elizacloud / plugin-local-inference.

### Mode matrix
| Mode (no `EMBEDDING_*`) | embeddings | Δ |
|---|---|---|
| cloud | plugin-elizacloud @50 | **identical** |
| local | plugin-local-inference @0 | **identical** |
| self-hosted Claude/Cerebras (opt in) | this plugin → any endpoint | **new capability** |

## Evidence (real, live)
Live-tested on a self-hosted **Claude-chat** bot (`provider=anthropic` throughout), same plugin, two providers by swapping one var:
- **OpenAI Direct** (`EMBEDDING_BASE_URL=https://api.openai.com/v1`): `[Embeddings] POST https://api.openai.com/v1/embeddings model=text-embedding-3-small` ×2 (store + recall), stored a memory, Claude chat unaffected.
- **Eliza Cloud** (`EMBEDDING_BASE_URL=https://www.elizacloud.ai/api/v1`): boot init resolved the cloud endpoint; a recall query returned **"Teal."** — correctly recalling the memory stored under OpenAI embeddings (same model/dim → compatible vectors). Zero embedding errors.
- 27/27 unit tests (null-probe width, wire-mocked vector, dimension-mismatch/empty/unsupported/no-endpoint/non-OK throws, batch ordering, `VECTOR_DIMS` contract), build + lint clean.

Screenshots/video N/A (server-side embedding plumbing; backend logs above are the evidence). Audio N/A.

## Follow-up (not this PR)
openai/openrouter/elizacloud ~95% duplicate a hand-rolled `fetch(${baseURL}/embeddings)` — a shared OpenAI-compatible embeddings client is a worthwhile separate cleanup.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)
36:Te29,## What & why

Today the home (`/chat`) is a fixed orange field and the Views catalog is a fixed light surface. This replaces both with **one persistent, user-set background** shared seamlessly across the home and Views — the React state lives at the shell root, so navigating never remounts or flashes it.

A new minimal, Apple-like **Background view** lets the user set it from three sources:
- **Shader color** — a curated swatch row + a custom color picker. The orange ambient is generalized so *any* color drives the same gentle rim pulse (the default stays the prior warm orange).
- **Image upload** — client-side downscale → a quota-safe data URL (no localStorage overflow).
- **Generate (cloud-gated)** — a prompt → AI image. The control is **hidden when cloud is unavailable**.

Apps/views can still paint their own background; only the home and Views opt into the shared one (Views through a frosted material so text stays legible on any wallpaper).

## How it's built
- `backgroundConfig` persisted preference (`{ mode: 'shader'|'image', color, imageUrl? }`), mirroring the `uiTheme` pattern (`localStorage` key `eliza:ui-background`) → exposed via `useBackgroundConfig()`.
- `AppBackground` mounted **once** at the shell root (`fixed inset-0 z-0`); content sits at `z-10`. Renders `ShaderBackground` (configurable color) or `ImageBackground` (cover).
- Cloud-gated generation: `POST /api/background/generate-image` → runtime media-generation service → persisted `/api/media/<hash>` URL; `client.generateBackgroundImage(prompt)`.
- Registered as a built-in view (agent `BUILTIN_VIEWS` + nav `BuiltinTab`/`TAB_PATHS`/`titleForTab`), so it appears in the Views catalog and is agent-navigable.

## Evidence

**Unit / component (18 tests, all green)**
- `state/persistence.background.test.ts` — normalize/load/save + defaults + corrupt-input handling.
- `backgrounds/AppBackground.test.tsx` — renders shader in the configured color, image mode, and the missing-config fallback.
- `components/pages/BackgroundView.test.tsx` — swatch → shader config; upload → image config; **Generate hidden without cloud / shown with cloud**; generate → client call → image config; error surfaced.
- `components/pages/background-image.test.ts` — non-image rejection + over-quota rejection.
- Updated `App.cloud-shell.test.tsx` to assert the root-mounted `AppBackground` + transparent home shell.

**Real-browser e2e screenshots** (`bun run --cwd packages/ui test:background-e2e`) — the real `AppBackground` recolors live (orange → teal → violet, same mounted layer, no remount) and renders a cover image. Plus `test:home-screen-e2e` renders the **real HomeScreen over the new shader** (0 page errors) — proves the home integrates unchanged in look.

**Stories** — `ShaderBackground` / `ImageBackground` (story-gate coverage).

**Static** — `bun run --cwd packages/ui typecheck` clean; Biome clean on all touched files. Agent route + client method typecheck clean.

### N/A (with reasons)
- **Video walkthrough** — no stable live UI dev server was available (a concurrent multi-agent edit-storm was actively reloading the agent), which would have produced an unreliable recording. Substituted with real-browser e2e stills of the live-recoloring background + the real-HomeScreen integration shot.
- **Real-LLM trajectory** — this is a UI/state feature with no action/prompt/model-loop change. The only model call (image generation) sits behind the cloud-gated endpoint and is exercised via the client in component tests.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)37:Tf29,Closes part of #9565 (boot/startup optimization epic).

## What

Optimizes the device agent boot/startup readiness path end to end, building on
the renderer-surface orange already landed by #9587 (which fixed only
`index.html` + `StartupShell`, left native launch configs at `#FF5800`, and set
`StartupShell` to `var(--bg,…)` which renders the theme white/black — see proof).

### 1. Measure — renderer startup trace + unified trace id
`@elizaos/ui/state/startup-telemetry` — a dependency-free recorder emitting
`performance.mark()`s at the documented cold-start checkpoints and mirroring them
to `window.__ELIZA_STARTUP_TRACE__`. It only **observes**; `useStartupCoordinator`
stays the sole startup authority and the pure reducer is untouched. The trace id
adopts a native-host-injected `window.__ELIZA_STARTUP_TRACE_ID__` when present
(one id across native host ↔ renderer ↔ backend boot), else derives a
renderer-local id + records `timeOrigin` for time-based correlation.

Checkpoints: `module-eval`, `main-start`, `app-modules:start/end`,
`bridges:start/end`, `react-mount:start/end`, `startup-shell:first-paint`,
`coordinator:<phase>` (incl. `coordinator:ready`).

### 2. Optimize — shrink the first-paint critical path
`initializeAppModules()` blocked the first React mount on **10** plugin imports;
the boot config reads only **3** synchronously. The other 7 (personal-assistant,
vincent, task-coordinator ×2, phone, steward, training) are imported for
side-effect registration / `React.lazy` pre-warm only → moved to the existing
idle path (`BOOT_CONFIG_DEFERRED_MODULE_LOADERS`). Measured `app-modules` span:
**~33–44 ms warm** (was a blocking await of 10 imports).

### 3. Launch orange = `#ef5a1f` (no flash)
Every boot/loading/launch surface now uses `#ef5a1f` (= `DEFAULT_BACKGROUND_COLOR`,
the home `ShaderBackground`) so native splash → React `StartupShell` → home is a
single seamless orange. `StartupShell` uses a dedicated `--launch-bg` token
(**proven** to render `rgb(239,90,31)` = `#ef5a1f`; `--bg` resolves to the theme
`rgb(245,245,244)` near-white, so develop's `var(--bg,…)` would flash near-white).
Native surfaces fixed: `capacitor.config.ts`, `app.config.ts` web colors + PWA
manifest, Android `colors.xml`/`styles.xml`, iOS `LaunchScreen.storyboard`. Brand
accent stays `#FF5800`.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)38:T9ed,Follow-up to #9455 / #9575. The NLMS echo canceller seam in `audio-frame-consumer.ts` was ready, but on the live device path nothing supplied the far-end reference and the playback→mic delay was never calibrated. This wires both.

## What this delivers (the three #9583 checkboxes)

- [x] **Wire the `echoReference` provider on the live device path.** New `PlaybackReferenceBuffer` (a bounded, timestamped ring of the agent's TTS playback PCM) + `createEchoReferenceProvider` factory, owned by `LiveDiarizationSession` and passed to the `AudioFrameConsumer`. The device feeds the far-end via a new `POST /api/voice/audio-frames/playback` route. When no playback covers a mic window the provider returns `null` and the canceller is skipped entirely (zero idle cost, exact passthrough).
- [x] **On-device playback→mic delay calibration per platform.** `echo-delay-calibrator.ts` estimates the bulk delay by normalized cross-correlation (GCC-style) over an echo-only window, with a per-platform seed table (`darwin`/`ios`/`android`/`win32`/`linux`). The session seeds from `process.platform` and auto-refines on the first echo-only burst; the active delay + confidence are surfaced in `/api/voice/audio-frames/status`.
- [x] **(Optional) AEC3-class residual-echo suppression.** Opt-in single-band residual suppressor on the canceller output, gated strictly to echo-only frames (far-end active, no double-talk) so it can never suppress a barge-in. Default off; enabled per device via `ELIZA_VOICE_AEC_RESIDUAL_SUPPRESSION`.

## Evidence

40 vitest tests, all green, typecheck clean:
- `playback-reference-buffer.test.ts` — write/read alignment, gaps→null, eviction, delay offset, **end-to-end** proof that buffer→provider→consumer→canceller cancels the agent echo by **>10 dB ERLE**.
- `echo-delay-calibrator.test.ts` — recovers a known delay (±2 samples, confidence >0.7), zero-delay, silence→0, and does **not** false-lock on uncorrelated near-end speech.
- `nlms-echo-canceller.test.ts` — residual suppressor is a no-op by default, further attenuates echo-only residual when on, never attenuates the user while the agent is silent, and does not crush the user during double-talk.

```
Test Files  4 passed (4)
     Tests  40 passed (40)
```

The actual playback-capture wiring (device-side) and per-device delay tuning still happen on hardware; this lands the reusable agent-side machinery + seams + auto-calibration so a device run just supplies frames.

### Honest API reality (documented in code)
- **z.ai/GLM** exposes **no** programmatic usage/quota endpoint (web-UI only) → `pollZaiUsage` makes no network call and returns *usage-unavailable* (never strands a healthy account) rather than inventing an endpoint.
- **Moonshot/Kimi** exposes only a **USD wallet-balance** endpoint (not quota %) → `pollMoonshotUsage` validates the credential + flags a depleted wallet; it can't populate a 0–100 %.
- **smithers**: forwarding a real `maxTurns` would change live agent behavior (needs real-LLM trajectory evidence per `PR_EVIDENCE.md`), so this PR pins current behavior + documents the gap rather than rewriting the loop.

3b:T117c,## LifeOps Slice 3 — promote cache-backed stores to `@elizaos/agent` runtime services

Executes **Slice 3** of the LifeOps extraction plan
(`plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/docs/lifeops-extraction-plan.md` §"Slice 3 —
Promote pending-prompts / global-pause / handoff to @elizaos/agent runtime
services"), per the owner's "decompose, no umbrella" decision (neutral `eliza_*`
serviceTypes).

The three PA-internal, cache-backed (verified zero-SQL) stores become first-class
runtime services, mirroring `KnowledgeGraphService` exactly (lifecycle +
`getService` accessor).

### What moved

**New `@elizaos/agent` runtime services** (`packages/agent/src/services/`):

| Dir | serviceType | Class | Accessor |
|---|---|---|---|
| `pending-prompts/` | `eliza_pending_prompts` | `PendingPromptsService` | `resolvePendingPromptsService` |
| `global-pause/` | `eliza_global_pause` | `GlobalPauseService` | `resolveGlobalPauseService` |
| `handoff/` | `eliza_handoff` | `HandoffService` | `resolveHandoffService` |

Each dir holds the **copied** store ops (`store.ts`), a `class XService extends
Service` over them (`service.ts`), and a barrel (`index.ts`). Bodies were lifted
verbatim from the PA store files; cache access uses the runtime directly (the
runtime is `RuntimeCacheLike`), matching the knowledge-graph service.

**Registration** — the three classes are added to `createElizaPlugin()`
`services[]` in `packages/agent/src/runtime/eliza-plugin.ts`, alongside
`KnowledgeGraphService`, and named-re-exported from `packages/agent/src/index.ts`
(KG pattern — kept out of the broad services barrel to avoid TS2308).

**PA delegation** — the three PA store files
(`plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/src/lifeops/{pending-prompts,global-pause,handoff}/store.ts`)
now re-export the canonical impl/types from `@elizaos/agent` and add a
`resolveXStore(runtime)` helper that prefers the registered runtime service
(first-wins dedup) and falls back to constructing the cache-backed store directly
when the service is absent. Both paths hit the same cache keys, so the fallback
is behaviorally identical. PA consumers — pending-prompts/room-policy providers,
the work-thread field evaluator, the scheduler, the `SCHEDULED_TASKS` action, and
the scheduled-task runtime-wiring — resolve through the helper.

**Inward-only law preserved** — `@elizaos/agent` never imports
`@elizaos/plugin-personal-assistant`; bodies were copied inward, PA's copies now
delegate. All runtime string literals (cache keys, serviceType strings) are
preserved.

### Test evidence

- `bun run --cwd packages/agent typecheck` → **0 errors**.
- `bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-personal-assistant build:types` → **clean**;
  PA `tsgo` = **475 errors == pre-existing baseline**, **0 in any touched file**
  (the baseline lives in unrelated files: `client-lifeops.ts`,
  `lifeops-routes.ts`, `connector.ts`, …).
- New focused unit tests (mirroring the KG/store tests), one per service —
  `packages/agent/src/services/{pending-prompts,global-pause,handoff}/service.test.ts`:
  **10/10 passing** (serviceType literal, lifecycle, store round-trip,
  `resolveXService` → null when unregistered).
- PA consumer tests exercising the changed call sites —
  `pending-prompts.integration`, `global-pause.integration`, `room-policy`,
  `thread-ops-field-evaluator`: **all passing**.
- Full PA unit suite: **539 passing**. The 8 failing files are **pre-existing,
  environment-only** and unrelated to this slice — 6 fail on a known PA-vitest
  resolution defect (`@elizaos/agent/security/access` subpath shadowed by the
  bare `find: "@elizaos/agent"` alias, triggered by `plugin-inbox/src`), 1 is the
  mobile-only AppBlocker Capacitor stub, 1 is a desktop-build UI text assertion.
  All fail identically on the parent develop commit and for a trivial direct
  `import("@elizaos/agent/security/access")` probe — i.e. independent of this
  change. The `decomposition-integration.test.ts` gate is one of the 6 caught by
  that same resolution defect; its actual invariant for this slice (no collision
